mysql> -- 120. Display customers whose usage exceeds their plan limit
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> SELECT
    ->     c.name,
    ->     p.name AS plan_name,
    ->     p.data_limit_gb,
    ->     u.data_consumed
    -> FROM customers c
    -> JOIN plans p
    ->     ON c.plan_id = p.id
    -> JOIN usage_logs u
    ->     ON c.id = u.customer_id
    -> WHERE u.data_consumed > p.data_limit_gb;

mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> SELECT
    ->     p.name AS plan_name,
    ->     COUNT(c.id) AS customer_count
    -> FROM plans p
    -> LEFT JOIN customers c
    ->     ON p.id = c.plan_id
    -> GROUP BY p.id, p.name;

mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> -- 181. Plans with customers and transaction count
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> SELECT
    ->     p.name AS plan_name,
    ->     COUNT(DISTINCT c.id) AS customers,
    ->     COUNT(t.id) AS transactions
    -> FROM plans p
    -> LEFT JOIN customers c
    ->     ON p.id = c.plan_id
    -> LEFT JOIN transactions t
    ->     ON p.id = t.plan_id
    -> GROUP BY p.id, p.name;
+--------------------+-----------+--------------+
| plan_name          | customers | transactions |
+--------------------+-----------+--------------+
| Home Basic         |         5 |           70 |
| Home Plus          |         3 |           33 |
| Home Pro Unlimited |         2 |           20 |
| Home Gamer Ultra   |         0 |            0 |
| Home Starter       |         2 |            6 |
+--------------------+-----------+--------------+
5 rows in set (0.00 sec)

mysql>
mysql>
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> -- 182. Plan-wise customer and revenue
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> SELECT
    ->     p.name AS plan_name,
    ->     COUNT(DISTINCT c.id) AS customers,
    ->     COALESCE(SUM(t.amount), 0) AS revenue
    -> FROM plans p
    -> LEFT JOIN customers c
    ->     ON p.id = c.plan_id
    -> LEFT JOIN transactions t
    ->     ON p.id = t.plan_id
    ->    AND t.status = 'Success'
    -> GROUP BY p.id, p.name;
+--------------------+-----------+---------+
| plan_name          | customers | revenue |

    ->     ON c.plan_id = p.id
    -> JOIN usage_logs u

mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> -- 192. Customer usage percentage
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> SELECT
    ->     c.name,
    ->     p.name AS plan_name,
    ->     u.data_consumed,
    ->     p.data_limit_gb,
    ->     ROUND(
    ->         (u.data_consumed / p.data_limit_gb) * 100,
    ->         2
    ->     ) AS usage_percentage
    -> FROM customers c
    -> JOIN plans p
    ->     ON c.plan_id = p.id
    -> JOIN usage_logs u
    ->     ON c.id = u.customer_id;

mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> -- 193. Customers with usage percentage above 90%
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> SELECT
    ->     c.name,
    ->     p.name AS plan_name,
    ->     ROUND(
    ->         (u.data_consumed / p.data_limit_gb) * 100,
    ->         2
    ->     ) AS usage_percentage
    -> FROM customers c
    -> JOIN plans p
    ->     ON c.plan_id = p.id
    -> JOIN usage_logs u
    ->     ON c.id = u.customer_id
    -> WHERE
    ->     (u.data_consumed / p.data_limit_gb) * 100 > 90;

mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> -- 200. Complete customer transaction summary
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> SELECT
    ->     c.id,
    ->     c.name,
    ->     p.name AS plan_name,
    ->     COUNT(t.id) AS transaction_count,
    ->     COALESCE(SUM(
    ->         CASE
    ->             WHEN t.status = 'Success'
    ->             THEN t.amount
    ->             ELSE 0
    ->         END
    ->     ), 0) AS successful_revenue,
    ->     COALESCE(SUM(
    ->         CASE
    ->             WHEN t.status = 'Failed'
    ->             THEN t.amount
    ->             ELSE 0
    ->         END
    ->     ), 0) AS failed_amount
    -> FROM customers c
    -> JOIN plans p
    ->     ON c.plan_id = p.id
    -> LEFT JOIN transactions t
    ->     ON c.id = t.customer_id
    -> GROUP BY c.id, c.name, p.name
    -> ORDER BY successful_revenue DESC;

+----+--------------------+--------------------+-------------------+--------------------+---------------+

mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> -- 201. Customers whose total transaction amount
mysql> -- is greater than the average customer transaction total
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> WITH customer_totals AS (
    ->     SELECT
    ->         customer_id,
    ->         SUM(amount) AS total_amount
    ->     FROM transactions
    ->     GROUP BY customer_id
    -> )
    -> SELECT
    ->     c.id,
    ->     c.name,
    ->     ct.total_amount
    -> FROM customers c
    -> JOIN customer_totals ct
    ->     ON c.id = ct.customer_id
    -> WHERE ct.total_amount > (
    ->     SELECT AVG(total_amount)
    ->     FROM customer_totals
    -> );
+----+--------------+--------------+
| id | name         | total_amount |
+----+--------------+--------------+
|  2 | Priya Sharma |         3995 |
|  3 | Arjun Reddy  |         6495 |
|  5 | Kiran Rao    |         3995 |
|  6 | Divya Menon  |         6495 |

mysql> -- 202. Customers who spent more than every customer
mysql> -- in plan 1
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> WITH customer_totals AS (
    ->     SELECT
    ->         c.id,
    ->         c.name,
    ->         c.plan_id,
    ->         SUM(t.amount) AS total_spent
    ->     FROM customers c
    ->     JOIN transactions t
    ->         ON c.id = t.customer_id
    ->     GROUP BY c.id, c.name, c.plan_id
    -> )
    -> SELECT *
    -> FROM customer_totals
    -> WHERE total_spent > ALL (
    ->     SELECT ct.total_spent
    ->     FROM customer_totals ct
    ->     WHERE ct.plan_id = 1
    -> );
+----+--------------+---------+-------------+
| id | name         | plan_id | total_spent |

mysql> -- 203. Customer(s) with highest total payment
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> WITH customer_totals AS (
    ->     SELECT
    ->         c.id,
    ->         c.name,
    ->         SUM(t.amount) AS total_payment
    ->     FROM customers c
    ->     JOIN transactions t
    ->         ON c.id = t.customer_id
    ->     GROUP BY c.id, c.name
    -> ),
    -> ranked AS (
    ->     SELECT *,
    ->            DENSE_RANK() OVER (
    ->                ORDER BY total_payment DESC
    ->            ) AS rnk
    ->     FROM customer_totals
    -> )
    -> SELECT *
    -> FROM ranked
    -> WHERE rnk = 1;

mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> -- 221. Customers whose total spending is greater
mysql> -- than total usage
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> WITH spending AS (
    ->     SELECT
    ->         customer_id,
    ->         SUM(amount) AS total_spending
    ->     FROM transactions
    ->     GROUP BY customer_id
    -> ),
    -> usage_total AS (
    ->     SELECT
    ->         customer_id,
    ->         SUM(data_consumed) AS total_usage
    ->     FROM usage_logs
    ->     GROUP BY customer_id
    -> )
    -> SELECT
    ->     c.id,
    ->     c.name,
    ->     COALESCE(s.total_spending, 0) AS total_spending,
    ->     COALESCE(u.total_usage, 0) AS total_usage
    -> FROM customers c
    -> LEFT JOIN spending s
    ->     ON c.id = s.customer_id
    -> LEFT JOIN usage_total u
    ->     ON c.id = u.customer_id
    -> WHERE COALESCE(s.total_spending, 0)
    ->       > COALESCE(u.total_usage, 0);
+----+--------------------+----------------+-------------+
| id | name               | total_spending | total_usage |
+----+--------------------+----------------+-------------+
|  1 | Ravi Kumar         |           2495 |       430.2 |
|  2 | Priya Sharma       |           3995 |       889.8 |
|  3 | Arjun Reddy        |           6495 |      1450.2 |
|  4 | Sneha Patel        |           2495 |       310.2 |
|  5 | Kiran Rao          |           3995 |         210 |
|  6 | Divya Menon        |           6495 |       580.2 |
|  8 | nsk                |            998 |       445.2 |
|  9 | Modal Payment Test |            499 |       280.2 |
| 11 | loki               |           1298 |      1120.2 |
| 12 | narasimha          |            200 |        11.4 |
| 13 | renuka             |            100 |           0 |
+----+--------------------+----------------+-------------+
11 rows in set (0.00 sec)

mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> -- 224. Customers whose latest transaction
mysql> -- amount is greater than first transaction amount
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> WITH ranked AS (
    ->     SELECT
    ->         customer_id,
    ->         amount,
    ->         ROW_NUMBER() OVER (
    ->             PARTITION BY customer_id
    ->             ORDER BY date ASC, id ASC
    ->         ) AS first_rn,
    ->         ROW_NUMBER() OVER (
    ->             PARTITION BY customer_id
    ->             ORDER BY date DESC, id DESC
    ->         ) AS last_rn
    ->     FROM transactions
    -> ),
    -> comparison AS (
    ->     SELECT
    ->         customer_id,
    ->         MAX(CASE WHEN first_rn = 1 THEN amount END) AS first_amount,
    ->         MAX(CASE WHEN last_rn = 1 THEN amount END) AS latest_amount
    ->     FROM ranked
    ->     GROUP BY customer_id
    -> )
    -> SELECT
    ->     customer_id,
    ->     first_amount,
    ->     latest_amount
    -> FROM comparison
    -> WHERE latest_amount > first_amount;
+-------------+--------------+---------------+
| customer_id | first_amount | latest_amount |
+-------------+--------------+---------------+
|          11 |          499 |           799 |
+-------------+--------------+---------------+
1 row in set (0.00 sec)

mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> -- 228. Rank customers according to total spending
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> WITH totals AS (
    ->     SELECT
    ->         c.id,
    ->         c.name,
    ->         SUM(t.amount) AS total_spent
    ->     FROM customers c
    ->     JOIN transactions t
    ->         ON c.id = t.customer_id
    ->     GROUP BY c.id, c.name
    -> )
    -> SELECT
    ->     *,
    ->     RANK() OVER (
    ->         ORDER BY total_spent DESC
    ->     ) AS spending_rank
    -> FROM totals;

mysql> -- 231. Top 3 spending customers including ties
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> WITH totals AS (
    ->     SELECT
    ->         c.id,
    ->         c.name,
    ->         SUM(t.amount) AS total_spent
    ->     FROM customers c
    ->     JOIN transactions t
    ->         ON c.id = t.customer_id
    ->     GROUP BY c.id, c.name
    -> ),
    -> ranked AS (
    ->     SELECT *,
    ->            DENSE_RANK() OVER (
    ->                ORDER BY total_spent DESC
    ->            ) AS rnk
    ->     FROM totals
    -> )
    -> SELECT *
    -> FROM ranked
    -> WHERE rnk <= 3;

    ->     SELECT
    ->         c.id,
    ->         c.name,

    ->     FROM customers c
    ->     JOIN transactions t
    ->         ON c.id = t.customer_id
    ->     GROUP BY c.id, c.name
    -> ),
    -> ranked AS (
    ->     SELECT *,
    ->            CUME_DIST() OVER (
    ->                ORDER BY total_spent DESC
    ->            ) AS percentage_rank
    ->     FROM totals
    -> )
    -> SELECT *
    -> FROM ranked
    -> WHERE percentage_rank <= 0.10;

mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> -- 237. Customer percentage contribution to total revenue
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> WITH customer_revenue AS (
    ->     SELECT
    ->         c.id,
    ->         c.name,
    ->         SUM(t.amount) AS revenue
    ->     FROM customers c
    ->     JOIN transactions t
    ->         ON c.id = t.customer_id
    ->     GROUP BY c.id, c.name
    -> )
    -> SELECT
    ->     id,
    ->     name,
    ->     revenue,
    ->     ROUND(
    ->         revenue * 100.0 /
    ->         SUM(revenue) OVER (),
    ->         2
    ->     ) AS revenue_percentage
    -> FROM customer_revenue;
+----+--------------------+---------+--------------------+
| id | name               | revenue | revenue_percentage |
+----+--------------------+---------+--------------------+
|  1 | Ravi Kumar         |    2495 |               8.58 |
|  2 | Priya Sharma       |    3995 |              13.75 |
|  3 | Arjun Reddy        |    6495 |              22.35 |
|  4 | Sneha Patel        |    2495 |               8.58 |
|  5 | Kiran Rao          |    3995 |              13.75 |
|  6 | Divya Menon        |    6495 |              22.35 |
|  8 | nsk                |     998 |               3.43 |
|  9 | Modal Payment Test |     499 |               1.72 |

    ->     SELECT

    ->     FROM plans p
    ->     JOIN transactions t
    ->         ON p.id = t.plan_id
    ->     GROUP BY p.id, p.name
    -> )
    -> SELECT
    ->     id,
    ->     name,
    ->     revenue,
    ->     ROUND(
    ->         revenue * 100.0 /
    ->         SUM(revenue) OVER (),
    ->         2
    ->     ) AS revenue_percentage
    -> FROM plan_revenue;
+----+--------------------+---------+--------------------+
| id | name               | revenue | revenue_percentage |
+----+--------------------+---------+--------------------+
|  1 | Home Basic         |    6986 |              24.04 |
|  2 | Home Plus          |    8789 |              30.24 |
|  3 | Home Pro Unlimited |   12990 |              44.69 |
|  5 | Home Starter       |     300 |               1.03 |
+----+--------------------+---------+--------------------+
4 rows in set (0.00 sec)

mysql>
mysql>
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> -- 239. Plan(s) generating highest revenue
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> WITH plan_revenue AS (
    ->     SELECT
    ->         p.id,
    ->         p.name,
    ->         SUM(t.amount) AS revenue
    ->     FROM plans p
    ->     JOIN transactions t
    ->         ON p.id = t.plan_id
    ->     GROUP BY p.id, p.name
    -> ),
    -> ranked AS (
    ->     SELECT *,
    ->            DENSE_RANK() OVER (
    ->                ORDER BY revenue DESC
    ->            ) AS rnk
    ->     FROM plan_revenue
    -> )
    -> SELECT *
    -> FROM ranked
    -> WHERE rnk = 1;

mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> -- 241. Average revenue per customer for each plan
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> WITH customer_totals AS (
    ->     SELECT
    ->         c.plan_id,
    ->         c.id AS customer_id,
    ->         SUM(t.amount) AS total_spent
    ->     FROM customers c
    ->     JOIN transactions t
    ->         ON c.id = t.customer_id
    ->     GROUP BY c.plan_id, c.id
    -> )
    -> SELECT
    ->     p.id,
    ->     p.name,
    ->     AVG(ct.total_spent) AS avg_customer_revenue
    -> FROM plans p
    -> JOIN customer_totals ct
    ->     ON p.id = ct.plan_id
    -> GROUP BY p.id, p.name;
+----+--------------------+----------------------+
| id | name               | avg_customer_revenue |
+----+--------------------+----------------------+
|  1 | Home Basic         |              1621.75 |
|  2 | Home Plus          |                 3096 |
|  3 | Home Pro Unlimited |                 6495 |
|  5 | Home Starter       |                  150 |
+----+--------------------+----------------------+

mysql> -- 243. Customers spending above their plan average
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> WITH customer_spending AS (
    ->     SELECT
    ->         c.id,
    ->         c.name,
    ->         c.plan_id,
    ->         SUM(t.amount) AS total_spent
    ->     FROM customers c
    ->     JOIN transactions t
    ->         ON c.id = t.customer_id
    ->     GROUP BY c.id, c.name, c.plan_id
    -> )
    -> SELECT *
    -> FROM customer_spending cs
    -> WHERE total_spent > (
    ->     SELECT AVG(cs2.total_spent)
    ->     FROM customer_spending cs2
    ->     WHERE cs2.plan_id = cs.plan_id
    -> );
+----+--------------+---------+-------------+
| id | name         | plan_id | total_spent |

mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> -- 244. Plans having at least one customer whose
mysql> -- total spending exceeds overall average customer spending
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> WITH customer_spending AS (
    ->     SELECT
    ->         c.id AS customer_id,
    ->         c.plan_id,
    ->         SUM(t.amount) AS total_spent
    ->     FROM customers c
    ->     JOIN transactions t
    ->         ON c.id = t.customer_id
    ->     GROUP BY c.id, c.plan_id
    -> )
    -> SELECT
    ->     p.id,
    ->     p.name
    -> FROM plans p
    -> WHERE EXISTS (
    ->     SELECT 1
    ->     FROM customer_spending cs
    ->     WHERE cs.plan_id = p.id
    ->       AND cs.total_spent > (
    ->           SELECT AVG(total_spent)
    ->           FROM customer_spending
    ->       )
    -> );
+----+--------------------+
| id | name               |
+----+--------------------+

|  3 | Home Pro Unlimited |

mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> -- 247. Customers whose usage record count
mysql> -- is greater than transaction count
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> SELECT
    ->     c.id,
    ->     c.name,
    ->     COUNT(DISTINCT u.id) AS usage_count,
    ->     COUNT(DISTINCT t.id) AS transaction_count
    -> FROM customers c
    -> LEFT JOIN usage_logs u
    ->     ON c.id = u.customer_id
    -> LEFT JOIN transactions t
    ->     ON c.id = t.customer_id
    -> GROUP BY c.id, c.name
    -> HAVING COUNT(DISTINCT u.id) > COUNT(DISTINCT t.id);
+----+--------------------+-------------+-------------------+
| id | name               | usage_count | transaction_count |
+----+--------------------+-------------+-------------------+

|  4 | Sneha Patel        |           6 |                 5 |
|  5 | Kiran Rao          |           6 |                 5 |
|  6 | Divya Menon        |           6 |                 5 |
|  8 | nsk                |           6 |                 2 |
|  9 | Modal Payment Test |           6 |                 1 |
| 10 | Vikram Verma       |           6 |                 0 |

    -> LEFT JOIN usage_logs u

mysql> WITH customer_avg AS (
    ->     SELECT
    ->         customer_id,
    ->         AVG(amount) AS avg_transaction
    ->     FROM transactions
    ->     GROUP BY customer_id
    -> )
    -> SELECT
    ->     p.id,
    ->     p.name,
    ->     p.price
    -> FROM plans p
    -> WHERE p.price > ALL (
    ->     SELECT avg_transaction
    ->     FROM customer_avg
    -> );

mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> -- 265. Customers whose total payments
mysql> -- are at least twice plan price
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> SELECT
    ->     c.id,
    ->     c.name,
    ->     p.name AS plan_name,
    ->     p.price,
    ->     SUM(t.amount) AS total_payment
    -> FROM customers c
    -> JOIN plans p
    ->     ON c.plan_id = p.id
    -> JOIN transactions t
    ->     ON c.id = t.customer_id
    -> GROUP BY c.id, c.name, p.name, p.price
    -> HAVING SUM(t.amount) >= 2 * p.price;

mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> -- 267. Customers whose total usage is above
mysql> -- average customer total usage
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> WITH customer_usage AS (
    ->     SELECT
    ->         customer_id,
    ->         SUM(data_consumed) AS total_usage
    ->     FROM usage_logs
    ->     GROUP BY customer_id
    -> )
    -> SELECT
    ->     c.id,
    ->     c.name,
    ->     cu.total_usage
    -> FROM customers c
    -> JOIN customer_usage cu
    ->     ON c.id = cu.customer_id
    -> WHERE cu.total_usage > (
    ->     SELECT AVG(total_usage)
    ->     FROM customer_usage
    -> );
+----+--------------+-------------+
| id | name         | total_usage |

mysql>
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> -- 268. Plan(s) with highest average customer usage
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> WITH customer_usage AS (
    ->     SELECT
    ->         c.id AS customer_id,
    ->         c.plan_id,
    ->         SUM(u.data_consumed) AS total_usage
    ->     FROM customers c
    ->     JOIN usage_logs u
    ->         ON c.id = u.customer_id
    ->     GROUP BY c.id, c.plan_id
    -> ),
    -> plan_usage AS (
    ->     SELECT
    ->         plan_id,
    ->         AVG(total_usage) AS avg_usage
    ->     FROM customer_usage
    ->     GROUP BY plan_id
    -> ),
    -> ranked AS (
    ->     SELECT *,
    ->            DENSE_RANK() OVER (
    ->                ORDER BY avg_usage DESC
    ->            ) AS rnk
    ->     FROM plan_usage
    -> )
    -> SELECT
    ->     p.id,
    ->     p.name,
    ->     r.avg_usage
    -> FROM ranked r
    -> JOIN plans p
    ->     ON p.id = r.plan_id
    -> WHERE r.rnk = 1;
+----+--------------------+-----------+
| id | name               | avg_usage |
+----+--------------------+-----------+
|  3 | Home Pro Unlimited |    1015.2 |
+----+--------------------+-----------+
1 row in set (0.00 sec)

mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> -- 269. Customers whose usage is above
mysql> -- their plan's average customer usage
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> WITH customer_usage AS (
    ->     SELECT
    ->         c.id,
    ->         c.name,
    ->         c.plan_id,
    ->         SUM(u.data_consumed) AS total_usage
    ->     FROM customers c
    ->     JOIN usage_logs u
    ->         ON c.id = u.customer_id
    ->     GROUP BY c.id, c.name, c.plan_id
    -> )
    -> SELECT *
    -> FROM customer_usage cu
    -> WHERE total_usage > (
    ->     SELECT AVG(cu2.total_usage)
    ->     FROM customer_usage cu2
    ->     WHERE cu2.plan_id = cu.plan_id
    -> );

    ->                PARTITION BY plan_id

    ->     FROM customer_spending

mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> -- 277. Latest usage record for each plan
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> WITH ranked AS (
    ->     SELECT
    ->         u.*,
    ->         c.plan_id,
    ->         ROW_NUMBER() OVER (
    ->             PARTITION BY c.plan_id
    ->             ORDER BY u.date DESC, u.id DESC
    ->         ) AS rn
    ->     FROM usage_logs u
    ->     JOIN customers c
    ->         ON u.customer_id = c.id
    -> )
    -> SELECT *
    -> FROM ranked
    -> WHERE rn = 1;

mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> -- 278. Customers whose latest transaction
mysql> -- is above their own average
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> WITH ranked AS (
    ->     SELECT
    ->         t.*,
    ->         ROW_NUMBER() OVER (
    ->             PARTITION BY customer_id
    ->             ORDER BY date DESC, id DESC
    ->         ) AS rn,
    ->         AVG(amount) OVER (
    ->             PARTITION BY customer_id
    ->         ) AS avg_amount
    ->     FROM transactions t
    -> )
    -> SELECT
    ->     c.id,
    ->     c.name,
    ->     r.amount AS latest_amount,
    ->     r.avg_amount
    -> FROM customers c
    -> JOIN ranked r
    ->     ON c.id = r.customer_id
    -> WHERE r.rn = 1
    ->   AND r.amount > r.avg_amount;

    ->         2
    ->     ) AS transaction_customer_percentage
    -> FROM transactions t;

    ->     ROUND(
    ->         COUNT(DISTINCT u.customer_id) * 100.0 /
    ->         NULLIF((SELECT COUNT(*) FROM customers), 0),
    ->         2
    ->     ) AS usage_customer_percentage
    -> FROM usage_logs u;

mysql>
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> -- 282. Plans where more than 50% of customers
mysql> -- have at least one transaction
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> SELECT
    ->     p.id,
    ->     p.name,
    ->     COUNT(DISTINCT c.id) AS customers,
    ->     COUNT(DISTINCT t.customer_id) AS transacting_customers,
    ->     ROUND(
    ->         COUNT(DISTINCT t.customer_id) * 100.0 /
    ->         NULLIF(COUNT(DISTINCT c.id), 0),
    ->         2
    ->     ) AS transaction_percentage
    -> FROM plans p
    -> JOIN customers c
    ->     ON p.id = c.plan_id
    -> LEFT JOIN transactions t
    ->     ON c.id = t.customer_id
    -> GROUP BY p.id, p.name
    -> HAVING COUNT(DISTINCT t.customer_id) * 100.0 /
    ->        NULLIF(COUNT(DISTINCT c.id), 0) > 50;
+----+--------------------+-----------+-----------------------+------------------------+
| id | name               | customers | transacting_customers | transaction_percentage |
+----+--------------------+-----------+-----------------------+------------------------+
|  1 | Home Basic         |         5 |                     4 |                  80.00 |
|  2 | Home Plus          |         3 |                     3 |                 100.00 |
|  3 | Home Pro Unlimited |         2 |                     2 |                 100.00 |
|  5 | Home Starter       |         2 |                     2 |                 100.00 |
+----+--------------------+-----------+-----------------------+------------------------+
4 rows in set (0.00 sec)

mysql>
mysql>
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> -- 283. Plans where average customer usage
mysql> -- exceeds plan price
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> WITH customer_usage AS (
    ->     SELECT
    ->         c.plan_id,
    ->         c.id AS customer_id,
    ->         SUM(u.data_consumed) AS total_usage
    ->     FROM customers c
    ->     JOIN usage_logs u
    ->         ON c.id = u.customer_id
    ->     GROUP BY c.plan_id, c.id
    -> )
    -> SELECT
    ->     p.id,
    ->     p.name,
    ->     p.price,
    ->     AVG(cu.total_usage) AS avg_usage
    -> FROM plans p
    -> JOIN customer_usage cu
    ->     ON p.id = cu.plan_id
    -> GROUP BY p.id, p.name, p.price
    -> HAVING AVG(cu.total_usage) > p.price;
Empty set (0.00 sec)

mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> -- 284. Customers who never used their current plan
mysql> -- in a transaction
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> SELECT
    ->     c.id,
    ->     c.name,
    ->     p.name AS plan_name
    -> FROM customers c
    -> JOIN plans p
    ->     ON c.plan_id = p.id
    -> WHERE NOT EXISTS (
    ->     SELECT 1
    ->     FROM transactions t
    ->     WHERE t.customer_id = c.id
    ->       AND t.plan_id = c.plan_id
    -> );
+----+--------------+------------+
| id | name         | plan_name  |

    -> WHERE NOT EXISTS (

    ->     FROM transactions t

mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> -- 286. Customer who has both highest total transaction
mysql> -- spending and highest total usage
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> WITH spending AS (
    ->     SELECT
    ->         customer_id,
    ->         SUM(amount) AS total_spent
    ->     FROM transactions
    ->     GROUP BY customer_id
    -> ),
    -> usage_total AS (
    ->     SELECT
    ->         customer_id,
    ->         SUM(data_consumed) AS total_usage
    ->     FROM usage_logs
    ->     GROUP BY customer_id
    -> ),
    -> spending_rank AS (
    ->     SELECT *,
    ->            DENSE_RANK() OVER (
    ->                ORDER BY total_spent DESC
    ->            ) AS rnk
    ->     FROM spending
    -> ),
    -> usage_rank AS (
    ->     SELECT *,
    ->            DENSE_RANK() OVER (
    ->                ORDER BY total_usage DESC
    ->            ) AS rnk
    ->     FROM usage_total
    -> )
    -> SELECT
    ->     c.id,
    ->     c.name,
    ->     s.total_spent,
    ->     u.total_usage
    -> FROM customers c
    -> JOIN spending_rank s
    ->     ON c.id = s.customer_id
    -> JOIN usage_rank u
    ->     ON c.id = u.customer_id
    -> WHERE s.rnk = 1
    ->   AND u.rnk = 1;
+----+-------------+-------------+-------------+
| id | name        | total_spent | total_usage |
+----+-------------+-------------+-------------+
|  3 | Arjun Reddy |        6495 |      1450.2 |

mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> -- 287. Customers whose transaction count
mysql> -- is higher than their plan's average transaction count
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> WITH customer_counts AS (
    ->     SELECT
    ->         c.id,
    ->         c.name,
    ->         c.plan_id,
    ->         COUNT(t.id) AS transaction_count
    ->     FROM customers c
    ->     LEFT JOIN transactions t
    ->         ON c.id = t.customer_id
    ->     GROUP BY c.id, c.name, c.plan_id
    -> )
    -> SELECT *
    -> FROM customer_counts cc
    -> WHERE transaction_count > (
    ->     SELECT AVG(cc2.transaction_count)
    ->     FROM customer_counts cc2
    ->     WHERE cc2.plan_id = cc.plan_id
    -> );
+----+--------------+---------+-------------------+
| id | name         | plan_id | transaction_count |
+----+--------------+---------+-------------------+
|  1 | Ravi Kumar   |       1 |                 5 |
|  2 | Priya Sharma |       2 |                 5 |
|  4 | Sneha Patel  |       1 |                 5 |

    ->     SELECT
    ->         p.id,
    ->         p.name,
    ->         COUNT(c.id) AS customer_count
    ->     FROM plans p
    ->     LEFT JOIN customers c
    ->         ON p.id = c.plan_id
    ->     GROUP BY p.id, p.name
    -> )
    -> SELECT *
    -> FROM plan_counts
    -> WHERE customer_count > (
    ->     SELECT AVG(customer_count)
    ->     FROM plan_counts
    -> );

mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> -- 289. Median transaction amount
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> WITH ranked AS (
    ->     SELECT
    ->         amount,
    ->         ROW_NUMBER() OVER (
    ->             ORDER BY amount
    ->         ) AS rn,
    ->         COUNT(*) OVER () AS total_rows
    ->     FROM transactions
    -> )
    -> SELECT
    ->     AVG(amount) AS median_transaction
    -> FROM ranked
    -> WHERE rn IN (
    ->     FLOOR((total_rows + 1) / 2),
    ->     CEIL((total_rows + 1) / 2)
    -> );
+--------------------+
| median_transaction |
+--------------------+
|                799 |
+--------------------+
1 row in set (0.00 sec)

mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> -- 290. Customers with spending above
mysql> -- median customer spending
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> WITH customer_spending AS (
    ->     SELECT
    ->         customer_id,
    ->         SUM(amount) AS total_spent
    ->     FROM transactions
    ->     GROUP BY customer_id
    -> ),
    -> ranked AS (
    ->     SELECT
    ->         *,
    ->         ROW_NUMBER() OVER (
    ->             ORDER BY total_spent
    ->         ) AS rn,
    ->         COUNT(*) OVER () AS total_rows
    ->     FROM customer_spending
    -> ),
    -> median_value AS (
    ->     SELECT
    ->         AVG(total_spent) AS median_spent
    ->     FROM ranked
    ->     WHERE rn IN (
    ->         FLOOR((total_rows + 1) / 2),
    ->         CEIL((total_rows + 1) / 2)
    ->     )
    -> )
    -> SELECT
    ->     c.id,
    ->     c.name,
    ->     cs.total_spent
    -> FROM customers c
    -> JOIN customer_spending cs
    ->     ON c.id = cs.customer_id
    -> CROSS JOIN median_value m
    -> WHERE cs.total_spent > m.median_spent;

mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> -- 295. Month-over-month revenue change
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> WITH monthly_revenue AS (
    ->     SELECT
    ->         DATE_FORMAT(date, '%Y-%m') AS month,
    ->         SUM(amount) AS revenue
    ->     FROM transactions
    ->     GROUP BY DATE_FORMAT(date, '%Y-%m')
    -> ),
    -> comparison AS (
    ->     SELECT
    ->         month,
    ->         revenue,
    ->         LAG(revenue) OVER (
    ->             ORDER BY month
    ->         ) AS previous_month_revenue
    ->     FROM monthly_revenue
    -> )
    -> SELECT
    ->     month,
    ->     revenue,
    ->     previous_month_revenue,
    ->     revenue - previous_month_revenue AS revenue_change,
    ->     ROUND(
    ->         (revenue - previous_month_revenue) * 100.0 /
    ->         NULLIF(previous_month_revenue, 0),
    ->         2
    ->     ) AS growth_percentage
    -> FROM comparison;
+---------+---------+------------------------+----------------+-------------------+
| month   | revenue | previous_month_revenue | revenue_change | growth_percentage |
+---------+---------+------------------------+----------------+-------------------+
| 2026-04 |    5194 |                   NULL |           NULL |              NULL |
| 2026-05 |    5194 |                   5194 |              0 |                 0 |
| 2026-06 |    5194 |                   5194 |              0 |                 0 |
| 2026-07 |    5194 |                   5194 |              0 |                 0 |
| 2026-08 |    8289 |                   5194 |           3095 |             59.59 |
+---------+---------+------------------------+----------------+-------------------+
5 rows in set (0.00 sec)

mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> -- 296. Month with largest revenue growth
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> WITH monthly_revenue AS (
    ->     SELECT
    ->         DATE_FORMAT(date, '%Y-%m') AS month,
    ->         SUM(amount) AS revenue
    ->     FROM transactions
    ->     GROUP BY DATE_FORMAT(date, '%Y-%m')
    -> ),
    -> growth AS (
    ->     SELECT
    ->         month,
    ->         revenue,
    ->         revenue - LAG(revenue) OVER (
    ->             ORDER BY month
    ->         ) AS growth
    ->     FROM monthly_revenue
    -> ),
    -> ranked AS (
    ->     SELECT *,
    ->            DENSE_RANK() OVER (
    ->                ORDER BY growth DESC
    ->            ) AS rnk
    ->     FROM growth
    ->     WHERE growth IS NOT NULL
    -> )
    -> SELECT *
    -> FROM ranked
    -> WHERE rnk = 1;
+---------+---------+--------+-----+
| month   | revenue | growth | rnk |
+---------+---------+--------+-----+
| 2026-08 |    8289 |   3095 |   1 |
+---------+---------+--------+-----+
1 row in set (0.00 sec)

    ->     c.id,
    ->     c.name,
    ->     t.id AS transaction_id,
    ->     t.date,

mysql> -- 298. Each customer's cumulative usage
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> SELECT
    ->     c.id,
    ->     c.name,
    ->     u.id AS usage_id,
    ->     u.date,
    ->     u.data_consumed,
    ->     SUM(u.data_consumed) OVER (
    ->         PARTITION BY u.customer_id
    ->         ORDER BY u.date, u.id
    ->         ROWS BETWEEN UNBOUNDED PRECEDING
    ->         AND CURRENT ROW
    ->     ) AS cumulative_usage
    -> FROM customers c
    -> JOIN usage_logs u
    ->     ON c.id = u.customer_id
    -> ORDER BY c.id, u.date, u.id;

mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> -- 299. Customers whose latest spending increased
mysql> -- compared with previous transaction
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> WITH ranked AS (
    ->     SELECT
    ->         t.*,
    ->         LAG(amount) OVER (
    ->             PARTITION BY customer_id
    ->             ORDER BY date, id
    ->         ) AS previous_amount,
    ->         ROW_NUMBER() OVER (
    ->             PARTITION BY customer_id
    ->             ORDER BY date DESC, id DESC
    ->         ) AS rn
    ->     FROM transactions t
    -> )
    -> SELECT
    ->     c.id,
    ->     c.name,
    ->     r.amount AS latest_amount,
    ->     r.previous_amount,
    ->     r.amount - r.previous_amount AS increase_amount
    -> FROM customers c
    -> JOIN ranked r
    ->     ON c.id = r.customer_id
    -> WHERE r.rn = 1
    ->   AND r.previous_amount IS NOT NULL
    ->   AND r.amount > r.previous_amount;
+----+------+---------------+-----------------+-----------------+

mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> -- 300. Complete customer intelligence report
mysql> -- =========================================================
mysql> WITH transaction_summary AS (
    ->     SELECT
    ->         customer_id,
    ->         COUNT(*) AS transaction_count,
    ->         SUM(amount) AS total_spent,
    ->         AVG(amount) AS average_transaction,
    ->         MAX(date) AS last_transaction_date
    ->     FROM transactions
    ->     GROUP BY customer_id
    -> ),
    -> usage_summary AS (
    ->     SELECT
    ->         customer_id,
    ->         COUNT(*) AS usage_records,
    ->         SUM(data_consumed) AS total_usage,
    ->         MAX(date) AS last_usage_date
    ->     FROM usage_logs
    ->     GROUP BY customer_id
    -> )
    -> SELECT
    ->     c.id AS customer_id,
    ->     c.name AS customer_name,
    ->     p.name AS plan_name,
    ->     p.price AS plan_price,
    ->
    ->     COALESCE(ts.transaction_count, 0) AS transaction_count,
    ->     COALESCE(ts.total_spent, 0) AS total_spent,
    ->     COALESCE(ts.average_transaction, 0) AS average_transaction,
    ->
    ->     COALESCE(us.usage_records, 0) AS usage_records,
    ->     COALESCE(us.total_usage, 0) AS total_usage,
    ->
    ->     ts.last_transaction_date,
    ->     us.last_usage_date,
    ->
    ->     CASE
    ->         WHEN ts.customer_id IS NULL
    ->              AND us.customer_id IS NULL
    ->             THEN 'Inactive'
    ->         WHEN ts.customer_id IS NULL
    ->             THEN 'Usage Only'
    ->         WHEN us.customer_id IS NULL
    ->             THEN 'Transaction Only'
    ->         ELSE 'Active'
    ->     END AS customer_status
    ->
    -> FROM customers c
    -> LEFT JOIN plans p
    ->     ON c.plan_id = p.id
    -> LEFT JOIN transaction_summary ts
    ->     ON c.id = ts.customer_id
    -> LEFT JOIN usage_summary us
    ->     ON c.id = us.customer_id
    -> ORDER BY total_spent DESC;
+-------------+--------------------+--------------------+------------+-------------------+-------------+---------------------+---------------+-------------+-----------------------+-----------------+------------------+
